# Order 0.6.0 design campaign plan

**Current-state authority:** §7 is the sole owner of current per-concern phase state; §18 is the sole owner of current G0-G6 gate state. Header/prose/START-END records are historical or procedural and must not be interpreted as current state.
**WorkItem:** `work_0f6d97bf-5c9e-4ed7-8f4c-2dd8806d4a21`  
**Lifecycle role:** supporting campaign-control and coverage document; **not** R2 authority, technical-design authority, P6 contract authority, or the P7 `implementation-plan.md`  
**Staging branch:** `docs/0.6.0-design-campaign`  
**Foundation baseline:** owned only by the §18 G0 row. G0 may be `PASS` only when that exact full commit SHA is an ancestor of the campaign branch `HEAD` and the campaign-state fitness sensor passes.
**Governing authorities:** `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`, `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT`, `GW-DOC-WORK-ITEM-DOCUMENT-SYSTEM`, `GW-DOC-SPECIFICATION-STANDARD`, `GW-P1-DOC-CANONICALITY`

## 1. Purpose

This document governs the complete Order 0.6.0 design campaign from prerequisite investigation through an adversarially reviewable implementation plan.

The campaign is intentionally concern-driven rather than screen-driven or implementation-driven. Its primary objective is to reconstruct, from canonical P2/P3 authority, the complete intended functional design that the 0.5.0 work was supposed to realize; then derive the 0.6.0 technical solution design and implementation plan that will actually realize that design. The released 0.5.0 application, its tests and its rendered UI are evidence only and never define what 0.6.0 must preserve. The campaign makes omissions visible by ensuring that every material product concern has an explicit investigation start, an explicit factual end, a functional-design disposition, a technical-design disposition and final implementation-plan coverage.

The campaign progression is:

```text
G0 — Phase 0 factual/governance baseline established
  |
  v
PHASE 1 / R1
complete concern inventory + evidence-first concern investigations
  |
  | G1/G2: coverage proven; every investigation factually closed
  v
PHASE 2A / R2
complete 0.6.0 functional requirements / functional design
  |
  | G3: all concerns functionally closed + horizontal integration PASS
  v
PHASE 2B / DESIGN
complete technical solution design / P4-P6 consequences
  |
  | G4: all functional obligations technically realized
  v
PHASE 3 / PLAN
final executable implementation plan
  |
  | G5: complete P1->P7 traceability, no orphan task or uncovered requirement
  v
PHASE 4 / R3
adversarial readiness review
  |
  | G6: READY_FOR_SIGNOFF
  v
explicit sign-off / implementation authority
```

Actual implementation is outside this campaign plan. It begins only after the canonical PLAN and R3 package has been explicitly signed off.

## 2. Canonical 0.6.0 work point

This WorkItem is the **single active campaign/control point for Order 0.6.0 design work**:

`work_0f6d97bf-5c9e-4ed7-8f4c-2dd8806d4a21`

All 0.6.0 prerequisite investigation, concern coverage, functional-design status, technical-design status, implementation-plan coverage and readiness status are tracked here or in this WorkItem's canonical lifecycle artifacts.

This designation is a campaign/work coordination rule, not a new semantic authority layer:

- permanent P1-P6 rules remain owned by their canonical `GW-DOC-*` / requirement / technical-requirement owners;
- `research.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-design.md`, `implementation-plan.md` and R3 remain the lifecycle-owned package artifacts;
- findings discovered elsewhere that affect the 0.6.0 campaign must be linked back to this WorkItem rather than spawning an untracked parallel 0.6.0 design stream;
- a separate WorkItem is created only where the lifecycle requires independently governed remediation or unrelated work; its relationship back to this campaign must be recorded here;
- no other plan, handover, prompt, review or branch may act as a competing 0.6.0 campaign status owner.

The current documentation index points to this WorkItem as the active 0.6.0 campaign. Git history/PRs remain delivery provenance; this WorkItem remains the design-campaign tracking point until the campaign reaches G6 and explicit sign-off.

### 2.1 Single-plan authority and precedence

For this WorkItem, `0.6.0-design-campaign-plan.md` is the **sole campaign-process authority**. It determines:

- investigation ordering;
- two-concern batch definitions;
- mandatory pair-audit procedure and current pair-audit status;
- concern START/END control;
- finding/dependency routing;
- per-concern campaign phase state;
- G0-G6 gate state;
- horizontal audit requirements;
- progression permission between R1, R2, DESIGN, PLAN and R3.

There is no second campaign plan.

The one canonical P7 implementation-plan artifact is `implementation-plan.md`. It does not compete with this campaign-control document: it owns executable task definitions only when PLAN is current and G5 can legitimately close. Until fresh G4/DESIGN closure and PLAN regeneration, its previous task content is stale evidence and has no implementation-authorizing force.

Document roles are closed:

| Document | Exclusive role | Must not be used as |
|---|---|---|
| `0.6.0-design-campaign-plan.md` | campaign process, pair audits, findings/dependencies, status/gates/progression | product specification, technical architecture, P6 contract, P7 task set |
| `0.6.0-concern-coverage-ledger.md` | concern/authority inventory evidence | semantic completeness proof, status owner, implementation plan |
| `research.md` | current R1 factual synthesis | campaign status owner, R2 requirement source beyond its facts, implementation plan |
| `requirements.md` | current R2 functional requirements | campaign status owner, technical design, P7 task plan |
| `solution-design.md` | current DESIGN technical solution | campaign status owner, P7 task plan |
| `implementation-plan.md` | sole current P7 task/change/verification plan after PLAN closure | campaign sequencing/audit authority, P1-P6 semantic authority |
| `readiness-review.md` | current R3 adversarial verdict/return | repair document, campaign status owner outside its verdict consequence |

No additional plan-like document may be created inside this WorkItem to own prerequisite completion, remediation, audit cadence, implementation scope or campaign status. Amend the existing owner instead.

### 2.2 Precedence against stale or external information

The following can provide evidence or provenance but can never independently advance, reopen, close or redefine the campaign:

- historical concern START/END records;
- stale R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN/R3 content retained for reuse;
- prior release behavior;
- current implementation/UI/tests;
- CI or PR status;
- PR descriptions/review comments;
- handovers or session notes;
- prompts;
- model/chat output;
- documentation from another WorkItem.

When such material conflicts with the current authoritative role owner:

1. it is classified as evidence of a possible defect, stale assumption or reusable prior work;
2. the owning canonical document is re-opened if the evidence is material;
3. the owner is amended or revalidated through the governed lifecycle;
4. the lower-authority material never overrides the owner merely because it is newer, detailed or executable.

Progression permission is derived only from the current-state owners declared in this document: §7 for per-concern state, §8.4 for pair-audit progression, and §18 for G0-G6. No prose elsewhere may synthesize a competing current state.

Any normative product behavior discovered or selected during the campaign must be written to the correct canonical owner/lifecycle artifact rather than stated here as a parallel specification.

## 4. No current-state fallback / no regression-baseline rule

The 0.6.0 campaign is an authority-driven design campaign. **Current application state, current UI composition, current implementation behavior and current design shape are not campaign outputs and are never a fallback definition of desired behavior.**

Current code, tests, rendered UI, historical design documents and prior releases may be inspected only as transient evidence to:

- prove a defect or contradiction against canonical authority;
- identify an existing capability that a later DESIGN decision may deliberately reuse;
- locate an ownership/boundary failure;
- determine migration/deletion consequences after the required future behavior is already established;
- prove that a claimed correction actually landed.

They must **not** be copied into this plan, `research.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-design.md` or another campaign artifact as a descriptive inventory of "how the application currently works", a preservation baseline, compatibility target, regression contract, fallback behavior or implicit requirement.

The governing direction is always:

```text
canonical product intent / authority
        -> factual gap or unresolved decision
        -> required 0.6.0 behavior
        -> technical realization
        -> implementation plan
```

and never:

```text
current application/design state
        -> preserve by default
        -> document as desired behavior
```

A concern investigation therefore records only:

1. applicable canonical authority and accepted intent;
2. contradictions, missing decisions, ownership defects or unproven obligations;
3. exact decision inputs required by R2/DESIGN;
4. cross-concern dependencies;
5. verification evidence sufficient to support the finding.

Implementation observations remain cited evidence behind a finding; they are not restated as product facts unless the same statement is independently owned by canonical authority. Absence of a finding does not mean "keep the current application"; it means the existing canonical authority is sufficient for that concern.

Any wording such as "preserve current", "keep existing", "match current", "unchanged behavior", "current design is correct", or equivalent is invalid unless it refers to an explicitly cited canonical semantic obligation rather than incidental implementation/design state.

### 4.1 Campaign-purpose precedence

The purpose of this campaign outranks transient evidence and delivery status. A PR/CI result, browser test, existing regression, current implementation behavior, historical design statement, concern status note or handover may not become a functional requirement, preservation contract or campaign gate by implication. Such evidence may stop the campaign only when it proves that the canonical authority, document-authoring contract or conformance machinery needed to reconstruct R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN is itself defective. Otherwise it is routed to the appropriate later design, implementation or delivery work and the authority-driven reconstruction continues.

Browser/UI tests become relevant to 0.6.0 acceptance only after reconstructed functional requirements and technical design establish the behavior they must verify. Existing browser tests do not constrain the reconstructed design merely because they describe or protect 0.5.0 behavior.

### 4.2 Exhaustive semantic and realization proof

The prior campaign pass established broad authority/concern coverage but did not prove the complete semantic content and realization of every applicable obligation. That failure invalidates the former G2-G6 closure and requires R1 revalidation before downstream lifecycle artifacts can be trusted again.

The campaign must distinguish three independent completeness proofs:

1. **Authority coverage** — every applicable canonical owner, stable requirement and normative section has been examined.
2. **Semantic/information coverage** — every material obligation has been atomized into its required information, hierarchy, work-kind/stage variation, interaction, state, transition, failure, recovery, negative behavior and acceptance consequence.
3. **Realization coverage** — every obligation has a complete producer -> carrier -> consumer path through the actual authoring/schema/prompt/state/contract/runtime/browser/presentation boundaries that apply to it.

For every material obligation, current realization evidence is classified only as `CONFORMS`, `VIOLATES` or `UNPROVEN`. `UNPROVEN` prevents factual closure.

Concern mapping and stable-ID mapping are necessary inventory controls only. They are not semantic-conformance proof. Likewise, `REFERENCE`, `no functional delta`, `no technical change`, `no implementation change` and `already satisfied` are evidence-backed verdicts, never defaults.

Where the canonical authority defines screen- or stage-specific information design, the audit must be screen/stage specific. At minimum the Workbench proof covers every applicable combination of workflow step, work kind, interaction condition, current-attention condition, material/currentness condition, current/historical selection and responsive class. The audit must prove the information producer, canonical carrier, presentation derivation, rendered hierarchy and valid action for each material value.

The existing campaign material is recovered selectively:
- canonical P1-P6 authority remains authoritative unless the audit proves a defect in that owner;
- G-F01 through G-F05 remain reusable foundation corrections subject to freshness revalidation;
- prior concern authority facts, evidence references and implementation observations remain reusable evidence;
- prior concern closure verdicts, grouped no-delta conclusions, exact DESIGN no-change conclusions, the ten-path T1 scope and the former R3 readiness verdict are not current premises and must be independently re-proven;
- all normative replacement content is written into the existing canonical lifecycle artifacts or permanent canonical owners. No parallel functional/design/plan documentation set is permitted.

## 5. Phase 0 entry gate — G0

G0 is the factual-baseline gate. It passes when all of the following are true:

- one exact Phase 0 candidate commit is recorded as the campaign baseline;
- the campaign branch contains that candidate's authority/governance state;
- the six Phase 0 advanced concerns resolve through stable IDs to their current owners and retain their historical V1 semantic sources;
- the current documentation-topology/architecture sensor passes;
- the authority/topology/document-authoring/conformance checks required to trust R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN production are green on the exact candidate; a failing build/browser/release/delivery check does not block G0 unless that failure itself proves one of those campaign-foundation mechanisms is defective;
- there is no unresolved foundational authority ambiguity known at campaign start.

PR merge state is recorded as delivery provenance but does not block fact gathering or document completion. There is no independent reviewer/second worker in this operating environment, so G0 must not depend on a fabricated third-party approval.

Phase 0 remains subject to downstream validation. If functional-design work exposes a foundational authority defect, the campaign stops at the affected concern, records the evidence, repairs the canonical foundation through the appropriate governed WorkItem, and then revalidates the affected concern before proceeding.

### 5.1 Campaign current-state ownership and mutation law

Current campaign state is deliberately single-owned and mechanically constrained.

- **§7 Master concern register is the only current owner of C01-C22 phase/overall state.**
- **§18 Phase/gate status register is the only current owner of G0-G6 state and the exact G0 foundation-baseline SHA.**
- document headers, narrative summaries, START/END records, historical checkpoint prose and handovers may record evidence or history but must not declare or override current campaign state;
- when G2 is `IN_PROGRESS`, the active R1 concern is derived from §7 rather than separately named elsewhere;
- a gate may not advance unless every preceding gate required by the campaign progression is `PASS`;
- G0 may be `PASS` only if its full baseline SHA is an ancestor of the campaign branch `HEAD`, no `OPEN_FOUNDATION` finding remains, and the campaign-state fitness sensor passes;
- a downstream foundational defect changes the affected gate/register state first, is remediated through the governed owner/WorkItem, and is integrated into campaign history by ancestry before that gate can return to `PASS`;
- copying equivalent files, citing another branch, recording a PR result or relying on prior-session prose is not evidence of branch containment;
- after any campaign-state mutation, run the permanent campaign-state fitness sensor and derive the reported state again from §7/§18.

This is a campaign-control rule owned here. It does not create a second product, lifecycle, architecture, contract or execution-state model.

## 6. Coverage model

The campaign uses two independent completeness proofs.

### 6.1 Concern coverage

Every material product/design concern must appear in the concern register in §7.

The concern register is derived from current P2/P3 authority, not from the current UI navigation alone. Before C01 begins, Phase 1 must prove that the register covers all current stable functional requirement families and all user-visible workflow surfaces.

If a missing concern is discovered later, it is added to the register with a new stable `Cxx` identifier before analysis continues.

### 6.2 Requirement-to-concern coverage

Phase 1 must establish a coverage ledger:

```text
stable P2/P3 requirement / concern
    -> one or more Cxx concerns
    -> investigation evidence
    -> R2 disposition
    -> DESIGN realization
    -> PLAN task coverage
```

No P3 requirement may remain unmapped. A concern may cover multiple requirements; a requirement may legitimately span concerns, but one concern must be marked primary owner for campaign tracking.

## 8. Concern investigation protocol

Every concern uses the same explicit START -> investigation -> END protocol.

### 8.1 START record

Before evidence collection for a concern begins, add a START record to the concern log in this document:

```text
CONCERN START: Cxx — <name>

Start baseline:
- exact repository commit:
- date:
- current phase: R1

Canonical owners:
- ...

Requirement families:
- ...

Upstream intent:
- ...

Known downstream consumers:
- ...

Incoming cross-concern findings:
- ...

In scope:
- ...

Explicitly out of scope:
- ...

Questions to close:
1. What does canonical authority require for this concern?
2. Is that authority complete and internally consistent for 0.6.0?
3. What exact gaps or decisions remain before future behavior is unambiguous?
4. Which adjacent concerns can invalidate this concern's closure?
```

A concern is then changed from `NOT_STARTED` to `IN_REVIEW`.

### 8.2 Mandatory R1 analysis dimensions

Each concern investigation must close:

1. **Intent** — canonical P2/product reason and required user outcome.
2. **Authority** — canonical owner(s), stable requirement identities and applicable higher rules.
3. **Authority completeness** — contradictions, missing decisions, ambiguous ownership or unproven obligations that prevent a complete 0.6.0 decision.
4. **Information ownership** — required canonical source facts and semantic producer -> carrier -> consumer ownership where the authority depends on them.
5. **Interaction obligations** — only the operator-visible behavior required or left undecided by canonical authority.
6. **State/transition closure** — required lifecycle/material/proposal/approval/task/provider semantics and any missing decision.
7. **Negative behavior** — prohibited behavior and failure semantics owned by authority.
8. **Recovery obligations** — blocker, retry, stale, cancellation and return-to-owner requirements.
9. **Cross-concern interfaces** — authority/decision dependencies imported from or exported to other Cxx concerns.
10. **Proof state** — obligation-level evidence sufficient to classify every material current realization as `CONFORMS`, `VIOLATES` or `UNPROVEN`, without turning observed implementation/design state into a campaign-owned baseline.
11. **Authoring/input/output path** — schema/template/prompt/context inputs, generated outputs and structural/semantic conformance needed to preserve the obligation precisely.
12. **Realization path** — complete producer -> carrier -> consumer flow through canonical contracts/state, core/daemon/shell, persistence/transport, browser projection and rendered/operator consumption where applicable.
13. **Information design** — exact primary, secondary and technical/on-demand information, including work-kind/stage distinctions and current-attention overrides wherever user-visible.
14. **Verification ownership** — the permanent sensor or semantic review needed to prove the obligation rather than merely the existence of its identifier.

Current code, tests, UI and prior design may be inspected to prove a finding, but their incidental shape is never recorded as desired behavior, preservation scope or fallback semantics.

### 8.3 END record

A concern investigation cannot stop informally. It ends with:

```text
CONCERN END: Cxx — <name>

End baseline:
- exact repository commit:
- date:

R1 verdict:
VERIFIED | GAP_FOUND | BLOCKED

Authority facts / decision inputs established:
- ...

Findings:
- Cxx-F01 ...
- ...

Cross-concern findings emitted:
- X-Cxx-Cyy-01 ...
- ...

Incoming findings resolved:
- ...

Canonical owners implicated:
- ...

Open product decisions for R2:
- NONE | ...

Open factual questions:
- NONE | ...

R1 disposition:
FACTS_CLOSED | GAP_FOUND | BLOCKED

Revalidation trigger:
- ...
```

`VERIFIED` means the factual model is closed; it does **not** mean no 0.6.0 functional change is needed.

No concern reaches R2 while it has an unresolved factual question that could materially change the functional decision.

### 8.4 Mandatory two-concern audit cadence

The campaign is executed in bounded two-concern batches. Completing two concerns at a lifecycle phase does **not** authorize work on a third concern at that phase. The exact prior two completed concerns must first be audited against the full detailed requirements for that phase.

This cadence applies independently to R1, R2, DESIGN and PLAN.

The pair sequence follows the dependency order in §10.2:

| Pair | Concerns |
|---|---|
| A01 | C01 + C21 |
| A02 | C22 + C02 |
| A03 | C03 + C04 |
| A04 | C05 + C06 |  | PASS |
| A05 | C07 + C08 |
| A06 | C09 + C10 |
| A07 | C11 + C12 |
| A08 | C13 + C14 |
| A09 | C15 + C16 |
| A10 | C17 + C18 |
| A11 | C19 + C20 |

A pair may be reordered only when a newly proven dependency requires it. The campaign plan must record the dependency evidence and amend the pair sequence **before** work begins on the affected pair.

#### Pair-audit progression law

For a given phase:

1. the two concerns in the current pair are completed under the normal phase rules;
2. the pair audit enters `IN_REVIEW`;
3. the auditor reopens the exact current artifacts/evidence for both concerns and performs every applicable general and phase-specific check below;
4. the audit returns `PASS` or `FAIL`;
5. `PASS` authorizes the next pair at that phase;
6. `FAIL` reopens the defective concern(s) at the exact owning phase and the same pair must be re-audited after correction;
7. a missing audit is progression-equivalent to `FAIL`.

No model, handover, status summary or later lifecycle artifact may claim progress in pair A(n+1) while pair An lacks a current `PASS` at that same phase.

The audit must be adversarial. It may reuse prior evidence, but it must not trust the two concerns' own completion assertions.

#### General pair-audit checklist

Every pair audit must prove, for **each** of its two concerns:

1. all applicable canonical owners and normative clauses were actually inspected;
2. every material obligation was atomized rather than hidden behind a requirement ID, concern ID or broad summary;
3. every mandatory phase dimension was answered with evidence;
4. evidence is current for the exact reviewed repository/document baseline;
5. implementation/UI/tests/history were used only as evidence and never as desired-state authority;
6. every producer -> carrier -> consumer interface required by the concern is accounted for;
7. every applicable state, transition, interaction, failure, recovery and negative behavior is explicit;
8. work-kind and workflow-stage variants are explicit where applicable;
9. every cross-concern dependency/finding has an owner and is not silently deferred;
10. reused prior material is classified and independently justified rather than inherited by status;
11. there is no duplicate/shadow canonical owner created by the concern artifact;
12. no material item is left `UNPROVEN`.

The audit also challenges the **interface between the two concerns**: assumptions exported by one must agree with facts/requirements/owners consumed by the other.

#### R1 pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, R1 pair audit must prove:

- complete authority coverage beneath stable IDs, including normative prose without a dedicated ID;
- complete semantic/information coverage;
- complete realization coverage;
- exact schema/template/prompt/context input and generated-output obligations where agentic authoring applies;
- exact canonical carrier/state/event/document ownership;
- exact core/daemon/shell/persistence/transport/browser/presentation path where applicable;
- Workbench primary/secondary/technical information hierarchy where user-visible;
- applicable workflow-step × work-kind × interaction × current-attention × material/currentness × current/historical-selection × responsive-class consequences;
- current realization classified only as `CONFORMS`, `VIOLATES` or `UNPROVEN`;
- zero material `UNPROVEN` items before `PASS`.

An R1 audit must fail if a concern merely proves what authority says but does not prove how the applicable obligation is currently realized or violated.

#### R2 pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, R2 pair audit must prove:

- every material R1 obligation has an explicit functional consequence;
- each material P3 obligation has exactly one valid `REFERENCE | DELTA | LOCAL` relationship where the v2 authority-hygiene schema requires it;
- `REFERENCE` preserves canonical ownership but does not replace explicit required 0.6.0 behavior;
- user-visible behavior, information hierarchy, interaction, state/action/transition, failure, recovery, negative behavior, acceptance, scope and exclusions are complete;
- work-kind/stage distinctions from R1 remain preserved;
- no implementation choice has leaked into P3 unless that choice is itself binding authority;
- no grouped `no functional delta` statement hides multiple unexamined obligations;
- zero unresolved product/authority decisions remain for the pair.

#### DESIGN pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, DESIGN pair audit must prove:

- every R2 obligation has an explicit technical realization;
- canonical semantic owner and affected boundaries are identified;
- pure/core derivation versus daemon/shell/UI effect ownership is explicit;
- complete data/control and producer -> carrier -> consumer paths are specified;
- schema/contract/persistence/transport/provider consequences are explicit, including explicit `no change` proof where applicable;
- existing capability to reuse and incorrect behavior/owner to remove or replace are explicit;
- necessary new concepts are justified and no DTO/service/repository/controller/shadow-model family is introduced;
- FCIS, SoC, DRY/canonical ownership, strict typing and minimum-change constraints are satisfied;
- failure/recovery and permanent verification ownership are explicit;
- `No technical change` is accepted only with positive evidence that existing P4-P6 realization and current implementation satisfy the complete obligation;
- zero material architecture choices are delegated to implementation.

#### PLAN pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, PLAN pair audit must prove:

- every DESIGN obligation maps downward to at least one exact task action or an evidence-backed no-change/no-code disposition;
- every planned action maps upward to accepted requirement/design authority;
- expected production, test/validation and documentation/contract surfaces are explicit;
- required deletions/replacements are explicit;
- dependencies and ordering are executable;
- architecture-feedback checkpoints are present where required;
- verification gates prove required behavior, preserved required behavior, architecture/governance, authorized scope and minimum sufficiency;
- task/change surfaces do not force duplicate responsibility or hide an unsigned architecture decision;
- no implementation worker must choose a material P1-P6 decision;
- exact task scope is not narrowed by an unsupported earlier `no change` conclusion.

#### Pair-audit record format

Every audit is recorded in this campaign plan using:

```text
PAIR AUDIT START: <R1|R2|DESIGN|PLAN>-Axx

Concerns:
- Cxx
- Cyy

Exact baseline:
- repository commit:
- artifact hashes/SHAs:

Prerequisite:
- prior pair audit: PASS | N/A for A01

Detailed checks:
- general checklist: PASS | FAIL with evidence
- phase-specific checklist: PASS | FAIL with evidence
- cross-concern interface: PASS | FAIL with evidence
- reused-material revalidation: PASS | FAIL with evidence

Findings:
- ...

Reopened concerns:
- NONE | ...

Unproven items:
- NONE | ...

PAIR AUDIT END: <R1|R2|DESIGN|PLAN>-Axx
Verdict: PASS | FAIL
Next pair authorized: YES | NO
```

A `PASS` without evidence against the detailed checks is invalid.

#### Current pair-audit status

This table is the sole current-state owner for pair-audit progression.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A01 | C01 + C21 | PASS | PASS | FAIL | REVALIDATION |

## 9. Finding and dependency discipline

### 9.1 Stable finding identities

Use:

- `Cxx-Fnn` — finding owned by one concern;
- `X-Cxx-Cyy-nn` — finding discovered in Cxx but requiring disposition in Cyy;
- `G-Fnn` — genuinely campaign-wide foundational finding.

A finding is recorded once at its primary owner and referenced elsewhere.

### 9.2 Cross-concern rule

When C06 exposes a problem in C11, C06 records `X-C06-C11-nn`; C06 does not redesign Approval while still investigating Research.

The C11 START record must list that incoming finding. C11 cannot close until it has explicitly disposed it.

### 9.3 Foundational defect rule

If an investigation exposes a Phase 0-class problem—ambiguous canonical ownership, broken reference lineage, shadow authority, impossible lifecycle topology, or another foundation defect—the active concern becomes `BLOCKED`.

The defect is remediated at its canonical owner through a separately governed WorkItem. After that remediation lands, all concerns whose evidence/authority depended on the changed foundation enter `REVALIDATION`.

## 10. Phase 1 — R1 prerequisite investigation

### 10.1 Phase 1 objectives

Phase 1 must produce:

- the proven complete concern register;
- P2/P3 requirement-to-concern coverage ledger;
- one START/END record per concern;
- concern-local findings;
- cross-concern finding ledger;
- evidence needed to support authority gaps and later design decisions, without recording application/design state as a baseline;
- a final `research.md` synthesis.

Phase 1 does not choose final UI composition, new product behavior, technical architecture, APIs, persistence models or implementation abstractions.

### 10.2 Investigation order

Use dependency order rather than visual navigation order:

```text
C01 lifecycle/product model
  -> C21 product identity/language/claims
  -> C22 project governance/authority integration
  -> C02/C03/C04 WorkItem/source/queue foundations
  -> C05 workspace/navigation
  -> C06/C07/C08/C09/C10 pre-signoff stages
  -> C11 Approval
  -> C12 Execution
  -> C13 Complete
  -> C14 shared materials
  -> C15 provider interaction
  -> C16 settings/readiness
  -> C17 recovery/current attention
  -> C18 responsive/accessibility
  -> C19 component/theme consistency
  -> C20 horizontal certification/coherence
```

This order may be adjusted only when an explicit dependency discovered in the evidence requires it. The change and reason are recorded here.

### 10.3 G1 — inventory gate

G1 passes before C01 substantive analysis when:

- every canonical P2/P3 requirement family maps to at least one Cxx;
- every governed user-visible surface/flow maps to at least one Cxx;
- every lifecycle stage maps to at least one Cxx;
- every governed productive provider/settings boundary maps to at least one Cxx;
- there is no known material concern outside the register.

### 10.4 G2 — investigation gate

G2 passes only when:

- every concern has an END record;
- every concern R1 status is `FACTS_CLOSED` or `GAP_FOUND`, never `IN_REVIEW`/`BLOCKED`;
- R1 pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- every cross-concern finding has an owning destination;
- no material factual question remains open;
- `research.md` synthesizes the concern evidence without duplicating permanent authority;
- R2 can begin from closed authority facts and decision inputs without reconstructing an application-state inventory.

## 11. Phase 2A — R2 functional requirements / functional design

### 11.1 Per-concern functional closure

R2 processes the same C01-C22 register.

For each concern, R2 records:

- current authority disposition: `REFERENCE | DELTA | LOCAL` as required by `change-v2`;
- required 0.6.0 user-visible/observable behavior;
- information hierarchy and interaction obligations where applicable;
- state/action/transition/failure/recovery behavior;
- explicit exclusions and non-goals;
- acceptance criteria;
- dependencies on other concerns;
- any permanent P2/P3 owner that must be amended.

A concern reaches `FUNCTIONAL_CLOSED` only when an implementer/technical designer would not need to invent product behavior for that concern and every applicable semantic/information obligation has an explicit 0.6.0 consequence. A `REFERENCE` disposition preserves the existing canonical owner; it does not permit the package to collapse that owner's required behavior into a generic "no functional delta" statement.

### 11.2 Functional-design ownership

Normative functional decisions belong in `requirements.md` and the appropriate permanent P2/P3 canonical owner.

This campaign plan records status and references only. It must not become the only place where a product rule exists.

### 11.3 G3 — functional-design gate

G3 requires:

- C01-C22 all `FUNCTIONAL_CLOSED`;
- R2 pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- all R1 findings disposed;
- all cross-concern functional dependencies resolved;
- one complete end-to-end user-flow walkthrough from creation through terminal result;
- all return/recovery paths included;
- complete P2 -> P3 coverage;
- no contradictory current-attention, lifecycle, material, approval, provider or settings rule;
- a horizontal functional integration audit returns PASS.

If G3 fails, affected concerns return to R2 or R1 as appropriate.

## 12. Phase 2B — DESIGN technical solution design

### 12.1 Per-concern technical closure

DESIGN consumes only functionally closed concerns and determines, as applicable:

- canonical semantic owner;
- pure/core derivation vs daemon/shell/UI effect ownership;
- data/control flow;
- contract and schema consequences;
- persistence/transport consequences;
- provider integration consequences;
- existing capabilities to reuse;
- behavior/owners to delete or replace;
- minimum architecture-preserving decomposition;
- failure/rollback behavior;
- permanent verification sensor ownership.

The design must preserve FCIS, SoC, DRY/canonical ownership, strict typing, no-OOP/no-DTO posture and minimum sufficient change.

A concern reaches `TECHNICAL_CLOSED` only when no material architectural choice is delegated to implementation and every functional obligation has a proven technical realization. `No technical change` is permitted only when DESIGN proves the existing canonical owner, complete producer/carrier/consumer path and current realization already satisfy the obligation.

### 12.2 Authority amendments

Any P4/P5/P6 change is made at its existing canonical owner. The design records stable IDs and exact semantic-version pins where the package requires a fixed snapshot.

No concern-local duplicate contract/model is permitted for convenience.

### 12.3 G4 — technical-design gate

G4 requires:

- every functional requirement has an explicit technical realization;
- DESIGN pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- C01-C22 all applicable technical columns are `TECHNICAL_CLOSED`;
- no duplicated semantic owner or transport/persistence/UI shadow shape is introduced;
- complete producer -> carrier -> consumer paths are specified for changed semantics;
- failure/recovery and negative behavior have technical owners;
- permanent verification consequences are defined;
- horizontal technical integration audit returns PASS.

## 13. Phase 3 — PLAN final implementation plan

### 13.1 Plan construction

Only after G4 may the canonical `implementation-plan.md` be finalized.

Every P7 task must provide:

- objective;
- dependencies;
- exact scope;
- explicit exclusions;
- acceptance references;
- expected semantic change surface;
- required gates;
- exact authority references;
- concern IDs covered.

### 13.2 Bidirectional coverage proof

Before PLAN can close, prove both directions:

```text
every required design obligation -> at least one P7 task
every P7 task -> one justified upstream design/requirement obligation
```

A task without upstream justification is scope creep.

A design/requirement without a task is incomplete implementation planning.

### 13.3 G5 — implementation-plan gate

G5 requires:

- all applicable concerns are `PLAN_COVERED`;
- PLAN pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- the complete task dependency graph is executable;
- task scopes do not overlap through duplicate semantic ownership;
- the union of task scopes covers the designed change surface;
- exclusions are explicit;
- the gate union covers the verification required by the reconstructed functional/technical design and each signed task surface; existing UI/browser regression tests do not become preservation requirements merely because they already exist;
- the plan contains one valid canonical `order-tasks` block;
- no implementation worker must choose between material design alternatives.

## 14. Phase 4 — R3 readiness and campaign closure

R3 is an adversarial review pass and treats R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN as untrusted. It does not assume a separate human or worker; separation is by governed stage and evaluation posture, not personnel.

### G6 — readiness gate

G6 passes only with `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`.

R3 must specifically challenge:

- concern-register completeness;
- whether any concern was closed without a START/END record;
- unresolved incoming cross-concern findings;
- P2 -> P3 -> P4/P5/P6 -> P7 traceability;
- authority/version lineage;
- minimum-change discipline;
- missing negative/failure/recovery coverage;
- implementation ambiguity;
- stale R1 evidence caused by later repository changes.

After G6 and explicit sign-off, this campaign document's status becomes `READY_FOR_IMPLEMENTATION`. Actual execution remains governed by the signed P7 tasks and normal Order execution/validation lifecycle.

## 15. Horizontal integration audits

Individual concern closure is necessary but insufficient.

Three horizontal audits are mandatory.

### H1 — post-R1 factual integration

Before G2:

- prove adjacent concern assumptions agree;
- prove all cross-concern findings have owners;
- identify facts whose evidence changed during later concern reviews;
- revalidate early concerns where necessary.

### H2 — post-R2 functional integration

Before G3, walk the complete product:

```text
install/readiness
-> queue
-> create/source
-> R1
-> R2
-> DESIGN
-> PLAN
-> R3
-> Approval
-> Execution
-> Complete
```

Also walk every valid upstream return, stale-authority path, blocker/retry/resume path, source-change path, provider failure path and settings-readiness recovery.

### H3 — post-DESIGN technical integration

Before G4:

- one owner per semantic concept;
- complete producer/carrier/consumer agreement;
- no DTO/shadow representation;
- no browser-owned business policy;
- no duplicate persistence/transport authority;
- no effect leakage into functional core;
- complete failure/recovery ownership;
- verification at the correct boundary.

## 16. Campaign tracking rules

### 16.1 Status updates

Update this plan after:

- each concern START;
- each concern END;
- each cross-concern finding creation/disposition;
- each concern reaching functional closure;
- each concern reaching technical closure;
- each concern gaining final plan coverage;
- each phase gate verdict;
- any foundational reopening.

Status updates record evidence and references; they do not restate normative design.

### 16.2 Evidence baseline

Each START/END record identifies the exact repository commit used for its evidence.

If a later authority/code change materially affects already-closed evidence, the concern moves to `REVALIDATION`. It cannot silently remain closed.

### 16.3 No skipped states

A concern cannot jump:

- `NOT_STARTED -> FACTS_CLOSED` without START/END records;
- R1 -> `FUNCTIONAL_CLOSED` without factual closure;
- R2 -> `TECHNICAL_CLOSED` without functional closure;
- DESIGN -> `PLAN_COVERED` without technical closure where technical design applies;
- any phase -> `CLOSED` while incoming findings remain unresolved.

## 19. Completion definition

The 0.6.0 design campaign is complete only when all of the following are true:

1. G0-G6 are PASS.
2. Every registered concern is `CLOSED` or explicitly marked not applicable with evidence.
3. No concern's **Overall** current state is `IN_REVIEW`, `BLOCKED`, `REVALIDATION`, `GAP_FOUND`, or carrying unresolved incoming findings; an R1 phase cell may remain `GAP_FOUND` as the closed factual record of a gap that was subsequently disposed.
4. Every R1 finding has a final disposition.
5. Every R2 requirement has one canonical owner and acceptance criteria.
6. Every functional obligation has a technical realization.
7. Every technical/design obligation has P7 implementation coverage.
8. Every P7 task has upstream justification.
9. Every mandatory pair audit for R1, R2, DESIGN and PLAN is `PASS`.
10. The final horizontal audits pass.
11. R3 returns `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`.

The campaign then hands one exact, traceable package to explicit sign-off. It does not authorize implementation by status assertion alone.

## 20. Derived execution-state view

No current campaign status is maintained in this section.

Consumers derive current state from §7 and §18 only. In particular:

- the active concern is the unique §7 concern whose applicable phase is `IN_REVIEW`, unless a §7 `BLOCKED`/`REVALIDATION` state explicitly stops progression;
- the current campaign phase is the first gate in §18 that is not `PASS`;
- no prose summary, handover, prior model output, START/END record or PR status may override those registers.

This section intentionally contains no manually maintained status projection.

### CONCERN START: C01 — Product operating model, scope, success criterion and lifecycle semantics

**Start baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**Campaign phase:** R1 factual investigation

**Canonical owners:**
- `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF`
- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`
- higher governing P1 rules resolved through `GW-DOC-SYSTEMS-GOVERNANCE-PYRAMID-V1`

**Primary stable requirement families:**
- `GW-P2-CONTROLLED-WORK`
- `GW-FR-001-GOVERNED-LIFECYCLE`
- `GW-FR-002-REPRODUCIBLE-AUTHORITY`
- `GW-FR-205-MONOTONIC-REACH-HISTORY`
- `GW-FR-228-FUNCTIONAL-STATE-TRANSITION-CLOSURE`
- `GW-FR-232-CLOSED-WORKFLOW-STATE-LANGUAGE`

**Upstream intent:** Order must turn work into an exact governed package and verified result without letting agent execution, UI state or implementation inference become alternate authority.

**Known downstream consumers:** C02-C20 and C22. C21 owns product-language/identity consequences separately and must remain consistent with C01.

**Incoming cross-concern findings:** none at start.

**In scope:**
- product boundary and non-goals relevant to the 0.6.0 workflow;
- desired lifecycle and legal transitions;
- reached-history semantics;
- exact-package/sign-off relationship;
- what constitutes a verified result;
- discovery/return/block/terminal semantics at the product level;
- contradictions between current P2 and P3 lifecycle authority.

**Explicitly out of scope:**
- final screen composition (C05-C19);
- final provider interaction mechanics (C15);
- technical architecture/contract choices (DESIGN phase);
- product identity wording/brand hierarchy (C21).

**Questions to close:**
1. Does current P2/P3 authority express one complete and non-contradictory product operating model?
2. Are all lifecycle stages, legal returns, terminal outcomes and sign-off/execution boundaries complete at the functional level?
3. Do current Workbench/state requirements introduce behavior that contradicts or silently extends the lifecycle owner?
4. What 0.6.0 functional decisions, if any, must be carried into R2 rather than inferred later?
5. Which C02-C22 concerns depend on any C01 finding?



### CONCERN END: C01 — Product operating model, scope, success criterion and lifecycle semantics

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE` owns the canonical WorkItem transition relation; lower layers must defer to it.
- The canonical WorkItem lifecycle is IDEA → R1 → R2 → DESIGN → PLAN → R3 → AWAITING_SIGNOFF → EXECUTION → COMPLETE, with explicit upstream returns and blockers.
- Investigation/Audit are WorkItem kinds with valid R1-terminal paths; they are not additional lifecycle stages.
- Post-signoff validation remains nested inside EXECUTION and does not create new WorkItem stages.
- Exact sign-off is the execution-authority boundary; COMPLETE is terminal.
- The only R2 inputs from this concern are the two bounded taxonomy/presentation ambiguities recorded below.
**Findings:**

- **C01-F01 — P2 lifecycle diagram conflates abstraction levels.** `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF §6` visually presents IDEA/R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN/R3/AWAITING SIGN-OFF together with TODO/IMPLEMENT/MECHANICAL VERIFY/INDEPENDENT REVIEW/CANDIDATE DONE as one linear “Desired lifecycle”. P3 correctly models the latter as post-signoff execution/validation internals under EXECUTION. This is not a state contradiction, but P2 does not explicitly state that the diagram changes abstraction level after sign-off.
- **C01-F02 — “stage” terminology is overloaded in lifecycle context.** `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT §6` is titled “Stage decision boundaries” and includes canonical WorkItem stages alongside IMPLEMENT, TEST / MECHANICAL VALIDATION, INDEPENDENT REVIEW and AUDIT. `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE` defines the canonical WorkItem stage set differently, and Audit is a WorkItem kind with an R1 terminal path. The intended authority ownership is recoverable from the documents, but the vocabulary permits unnecessary inference.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:**

- **X-C01-C22-01 → C22:** project authority/context resolution must use an explicit taxonomy that distinguishes canonical WorkItem lifecycle stage, WorkItem kind, execution role and validation boundary. C22 cannot close while this distinction remains ambiguous.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Canonical owners implicated:**

- `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF`
- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`
- `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT`
- `GW-DOC-WORKBENCH-UX`
- `GW-DOC-VALIDATION-STANDARD-V1`

**Open product decisions for R2:**

1. minimum amendment to P2's desired-lifecycle presentation so canonical WorkItem stages and nested execution/validation flow are explicit;
2. exact closed terminology for WorkItem stage vs WorkItem kind vs execution role vs validation boundary, propagated to lifecycle-context consumers.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND` — facts are closed; two bounded documentation/functional-taxonomy gaps require R2 disposition.

**Revalidation trigger:** any change to the canonical WorkItem transition relation, product success criterion, execution validation topology, or lifecycle-context decision-boundary taxonomy.

### CONCERN START: C21 — Product identity, product language, claim boundaries, naming, brand/family positioning

**Start baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**Campaign phase:** R1 factual investigation

**Canonical owners:**
- `GW-DOC-PRODUCT-IDENTITY-V1`
- `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF`

**Primary authority areas:**
- canonical product name/category/proposition;
- user-level value and differentiation;
- approved product-language hierarchy;
- supporting messages;
- claim boundaries;
- visual identity;
- Pontivus/Span family relationship;
- positioning/naming boundaries.

**Upstream intent:** product language must accurately describe the governed software-work control model established by C01 without inventing capabilities or exposing implementation terminology as product meaning.

**Known downstream consumers:** C01, C05, C19 and public/documentation surfaces.

**Incoming cross-concern findings:** none at start.

**In scope:**
- factual consistency of identity/positioning claims with canonical product authority;
- whether product language still describes the actual governed lifecycle and current capabilities;
- whether claims overstate autonomy, verification, review, delivery or multi-agent behavior;
- consistency between product identity and System Brief.

**Explicitly out of scope:**
- redesigning the visual system (C19);
- changing lifecycle behavior (C01/R2);
- implementation mechanics.

**Questions to close:**
1. Is every approved product claim supported by current P2/P3 behavior?
2. Do naming/category/value statements conflict with the canonical product boundary or non-goals?
3. Does “verified”, “independent”, “software factory”, or agent-related wording imply actors/capabilities the product does not actually guarantee?
4. Is the Order/Span family boundary clear and non-authoritative where required?
5. What product-language decisions, if any, must R2 carry forward?



### CONCERN END: C21 — Product identity, product language, claim boundaries, naming, brand/family positioning

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- `GW-DOC-PRODUCT-IDENTITY` bounds Order as a governed software-work control product, not an autonomous product manager, coding agent, CI/CD replacement, deployment authority or merge authority.
- Product intent remains human/project authority: Order governs how authorized work is carried through and must not invent missing product intent.
- Quantified outcome claims require external evidence and are otherwise prohibited.
- Span may supply context/tooling but owns none of Order's lifecycle, approval, execution or verification semantics.
- The R2 inputs are limited to terminal-result terminology and reusable product wording that currently permits over-broad interpretation.
**Findings:**

- **C21-F01 — terminal product outcome is too narrowly named.** Product identity §1 declares **Verified candidate** as the singular “Terminal product outcome”, while §2/§6 and canonical lifecycle authority explicitly admit valid terminal Investigation/Audit complete, disproven, duplicate and already-satisfied outcomes with no candidate. The broader proposition “verified result” can cover both, but the identity table currently collapses the umbrella terminal result into the delivery-specific candidate outcome.
- **C21-F02 — some expanded product wording overstates Order's decision role / delivery path.** The expanded description says “Order works out what needs to happen”, while lifecycle authority says missing P2 intent must block rather than be invented. The standard description also reads as though all work passes through implementation/testing/review, despite explicit no-code terminal behavior elsewhere. The surrounding document corrects both implications, but the canonical reusable phrases themselves are ambiguous.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:**

- **X-C21-C01-01 → C01/R2:** functional/product wording must preserve the distinction between a general verified terminal result and a delivery-specific verified candidate, and must not imply that Order originates missing product intent. C01 R1 facts already establish these semantics, so no factual re-open is required.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Canonical owners implicated:**
- `GW-DOC-PRODUCT-IDENTITY`
- `GW-DOC-SYSTEM-BRIEF`
- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`

**Open product decisions for R2:**

1. exact canonical terminology for umbrella terminal **result** versus implementation **candidate**;
2. minimum changes to reusable product-language hierarchy so it describes definition/execution “as applicable” and does not imply autonomous product-intent invention.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** any change to product outcome taxonomy, P2 product boundary, or the approved product-language hierarchy.

### CONCERN END: C02 — WorkItem intake, kinds, creation and metadata ownership

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- `GW-DOC-WORK-ITEM-DOCUMENT-SYSTEM` owns WorkItem schema/kind, portable source identity/classification, authoring metadata ownership and linked-remediation lineage.
- WorkItem kind is derived from the pinned schema; ordinary authoring must not create duplicate mutable kind/lifecycle/role/gate owners.
- The portable Work request remains the canonical originating intent source; runtime persistence is projection only.
- Linked remediation is same-project, bounded to the permitted parent/child kinds and immutable lineage semantics.
- R2 decisions are limited to ordinary concern presentation and same-project linked-remediation authoring; C02-F03 is a DESIGN ownership issue, not a product fallback requirement.
**Findings:**

- **C02-F01 — technical identifier leakage in ordinary Create.** `CreateWorkItem.tsx` renders the ordinary field label **Concern IDs** and raw identifier options. Functional authoring calls this **Concerns**, and Workbench information hierarchy classifies raw IDs as technical information. Selection is catalog-controlled rather than free text, but the presentation still exposes implementation identity as ordinary product language.
- **C02-F02 — linked remediation exposes an invalid Project choice.** The linked-remediation flow initializes the parent project but renders the same editable Project Selector. Canonical lineage is same-project only; changing Project can never succeed and is rejected only by the daemon. The browser therefore exposes an action space containing a known-invalid lineage choice.
- **C02-F03 — productive ingestion ownership is not singular.** Canonical source-consumer closure requires all authored WorkItems to enter through one productive ingestion/reconciliation owner. Implementation evidence proves that obligation is not yet realized. DESIGN must consolidate ownership without inventing a second representation.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:**

- **X-C02-C04-01 → C04:** the source/reconciliation investigation must determine the single productive ingestion owner and eliminate duplicated UI-create registration semantics without creating a new source authority.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Open product decisions for R2:**

- C02-F01/F02 require no new domain model; R2 should preserve existing canonical semantics and make the ordinary interaction unambiguous.
- C02-F03 is primarily a DESIGN/implementation ownership issue; R2 should reference the existing source-backed creation requirement rather than invent another behavior.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to schema catalog ownership/defaults, creation contract, WorkItem metadata authority, or remediation lineage.

### CONCERN END: C03 — Queue, repository discovery/reconciliation, active/archive visibility and work selection

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- Queue/discovery behavior is governed by portable-source reconciliation and repository-native archive semantics, not by a second installation-local work registry.
- Discovery/reconciliation must deterministically classify valid, invalid, missing, duplicate, misplaced and changed sources without fabricating lifecycle/run/approval history.
- Archive is separate from lifecycle state and must preserve WorkItem identity/history.
- Productive boundaries must observe the authoritative reconciled source rather than a stale projection.
- The only R2 gap is C03-F01: active filters/search must remain semantically consistent when archived work is included.
**Finding:**

- **C03-F01 — archived inclusion violates canonical queue-filter semantics.** The authority requires archive to alter membership visibility without creating a second queue/filter meaning. Implementation evidence proves project/search filtering is not preserved when archived work is included. R2 must close the required semantic outcome; incidental UI composition is not a design baseline.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** none.

**Incoming findings resolved:** none.

**Open product decisions for R2:** preserve one queue filtering/search semantics when archived items are included. This does not require new persistent state.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to queue visibility ownership, repository configuration/disconnect semantics, reconciliation dispositions or archive projection.

### CONCERN END: C04 — WorkItem source, title, portable metadata, authoring, document Git and repository durability

**End baseline:** `84c882473d202ff3866e6a0dd11c3a1df2fbff4d`  
**R1 verdict:** `GAP_FOUND`

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**

- The Work request file is the single portable source owner for originating intent, metadata and title; editing must not create parallel hidden authority.
- Source and lifecycle-document mutations are freshness/hash protected and must route through their canonical owning stage/authority reactions.
- Rediscovered lifecycle Markdown is documentary material only; file existence cannot fabricate workflow history or conformance.
- Documentation Git persistence is an explicit operator action over the governed WorkItem document set, separate from editor save.
- C04-F01 is a DESIGN ownership defect: UI creation and repository reconciliation must converge on one existing source-admission/reconciliation owner, without a new model or DTO.
**Finding:**

- **C04-F01 — productive source admission lacks one canonical owner.** Source-consumer closure requires one ingestion/reconciliation owner for all source-backed WorkItems. Implementation evidence confirms the ownership defect identified by C02-F03 / X-C02-C04-01. DESIGN must consolidate around the existing canonical owner, not preserve either implementation path by default.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** none beyond the DESIGN consequence already owned by C04-F01.

**Incoming findings resolved:**
- **X-C02-C04-01:** confirmed and consolidated into C04-F01.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE. Existing canonical source-authoring, freshness and documentation-Git authority is sufficient; no additional R2 product delta is required.

**Open technical decision for DESIGN:** consolidate initial source registration through the same canonical reconciliation/admission path used for repository-authored WorkItems while preserving exact creation semantics.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to source parser/writer ownership, source mutation admission, lifecycle-artifact edit semantics, or documentation Git ownership.

### G0 downstream revalidation note — v2 R1 template authority lineage

C06 discovered that current `change-v2` and `defect-v2` WorkItems were routed to historical `change-r1-v1` / `defect-r1-v1` templates. Because `stagePrompt()` injects the template verbatim as the **Canonical stage template**, current v2 Research received a historical `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT-V1` authority pin alongside current resolved authority.

This is a Phase-0-class lineage defect, not a C06 product-design decision.

The first correction exposed the same failure class for ordinary Investigation/Audit authoring: both kinds were still defaulting to historical `investigation-v1` / `audit-v1` pins. Phase 0 therefore also adds current `investigation-v2` / `audit-v2` defaults with unchanged intake/R1 structure and terminal outcomes, current lifecycle-context V2 authority, and no downstream delivery stages.

The Phase 0 package now:
- retains v1 R1 templates unchanged for v1-pinned WorkItems;
- adds `change-r1-v2` / `defect-r1-v2` with the same evidence structure and `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT-V2`;
- routes only current delivery v2 schemas to those current R1 templates;
- adds a permanent core regression test;
- documents the rule in `GW-DOC-WORK-ITEM-DOCUMENT-SYSTEM-V2` and the Phase 0 WorkItem.

| ID | Required semantic obligation | Realization evidence | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|

| C05-O02 | Current, reached/historical and unreached are derived from canonical history rather than stage ordinal or document existence | WorkItem + Runs/accepted completion events/tasks -> pure reached set; repository draft artifacts are deliberately excluded from reach derivation | CONFORMS |

| C05-O04 | Context is explicit, bounded and secondary; it does not auto-open or become a competing workflow/material navigator | root `contextOpen=false` + explicit Context action -> WorkItemContext/drawer; browser coverage verifies no automatic opening and bounded behavior | CONFORMS |

| C05-O09 | Current material/history selection does not create mutation eligibility; authoring is disabled during active Runs and by canonical stage/currentness rules | canonical attention/Run state -> mutation disable + DocumentWorkspace editability; daemon independently enforces | CONFORMS |

| C05-O11 | Asynchronous terminalization drains final events and refetches the full dependent observation before new actions are trusted | active Run polling + terminal event drain + dependent query refetch -> coherence/current attention | CONFORMS |

**Finding C05-F01:** explicit current-step material selection has higher resolution precedence than current required-input/proposal/blocker ownership. A status label can remain visible, but the exact interaction is not guaranteed to remain visible/directly actionable. This violates the canonical attention hierarchy without implying a need for new state.

### G0 downstream revalidation note — v2 DESIGN template completeness

C08 identified a second downstream validation defect in the Phase 0 authoring foundation. The current Change/Defect v2 DESIGN templates were structurally narrower than the binding `GW-DOC-SPECIFICATION-STANDARD` S7 contract: they did not explicitly require existing-capability/reuse analysis, behavior to replace/delete, necessary-new-concept justification or rejected alternatives/rationale.

Because these schema templates are injected into productive DESIGN prompts and define the required document sections, the omission is a foundational authoring-contract defect rather than a 0.6.0 product-design decision.

The Phase 0 correction:
- extends both current v2 DESIGN templates to carry the complete S7 content contract;
- preserves historical v1 design templates unchanged;
- aligns the WorkItem-document and canonical-contract owners;
- adds permanent regression coverage.

**Corrected Phase 0 candidate:** `d066b3b6fc1347909d11ea5aa376014801081fd5`.

C01-C07 facts remain valid. The DESIGN-template correction is verified by the campaign-relevant test suite on `732cfb66`; C08 remains open and resumes from its existing START record.


### G0 downstream revalidation note — mechanical readiness timing

The foundation audit identified that complete signed-task mechanical validation was performed only by the Approval/sign-off package path after R3 had already returned `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`. This contradicted the binding requirement that mechanically checkable readiness be closed before readiness promotion.

The Phase 0 correction reuses the existing `validateTaskDefinitions` owner at the R3 coordinator boundary only for `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`, using the exact current PLAN and current material closure. Invalid gates, concerns, authority/acceptance refs, scope/exclusions or signed-material change surfaces now block R3 conformance. Return-to-* outcomes remain available so R3 can route a defective package upstream rather than being trapped by the defect it is reporting.

A daemon regression exercises the real provider→R3 completion path.

**Corrected Phase 0 candidate:** `732cfb66ab1b71ad1454b69e668cb7641e140988`.

### CONCERN START: C09 — PLAN-stage interaction, task-definition authoring and plan inspection

**Start baseline:** `3a74ca53250eabf6087c6bd67e20e1151d08ba5a`  
**Campaign phase:** R1 factual investigation

**Canonical owners:**
- `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`
- `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT`
- `GW-DOC-SPECIFICATION-STANDARD`
- `GW-DOC-CANONICAL-CONTRACTS`
- `GW-DOC-PROJECT-GOVERNANCE-INTEGRATION`
- `GW-DOC-WORKBENCH-UX-V2`
- `GW-DOC-WORK-ITEM-DOCUMENT-SYSTEM`.

**In scope:**
- PLAN decision boundary and exact minimum repository transition;
- requirement/design-to-task traceability;
- signed task-definition authoring and canonical `order-tasks` ownership;
- dependency order, expected change surface, deletions/replacements and verification contract;
- project verification-authority resolution;
- governed implementation-plan document interaction and inspection;
- PLAN conformance/promotion.

**Explicitly out of scope:** choosing new product behavior (C07/R2), choosing unresolved architecture/contracts (C08/DESIGN), readiness adjudication (C10), and actual implementation/execution (C12).

**Questions to close:**
1. Does PLAN receive complete accepted P1-P6/design authority and prohibit new requirements/architecture/contracts?
2. Must every executable task trace upward to resolved authority and every required outcome trace downward to a task or explicit no-code disposition?
3. Is the canonical signed-task definition singular and structurally parsed before PLAN promotion, with complete mechanically resolvable task-definition validation performed no later than R3 `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`?
4. Are expected change surface, required deletions/replacements, dependency order and validation gates complete without becoming arbitrary implementation detail?
5. Is project verification authority resolved rather than substituted by PLAN or implementation?
6. Is implementation-plan interaction/document ownership coherent in the Workbench?
7. What authority or implementation violations require later functional/technical remediation?


### C09 foundational finding — G-F04 current-v2 PLAN authoring-contract completeness

Canonical authority is unambiguous:

- `GW-DOC-SPECIFICATION-STANDARD` S8 requires each implementation-plan task to identify objective, requirement/design traceability, expected production surface, module ownership/decomposition constraints, expected test/validation surface, required deletions/replacements, dependencies and completion evidence, including architecture-feedback checkpoints where a task contains ordered architecture-affecting steps.
- S9 requires an explicit minimum-change forecast covering production, test/gate and documentation/contract surfaces plus any expected new state/schema/dependency/configuration/migration/abstraction/API.
- S10 requires the logical verification contract proving required behavior, prior required behavior, architecture/governance, authorized scope and minimum sufficiency.
- `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT` requires PLAN to produce the P7 implementation plan, explicit execution-task definitions, change-surface forecast and verification contract without inventing new P1-P6 decisions.

The current v2 PLAN templates do not bind that complete content contract. `change-plan-v2` requires only exact task units/dependencies, traceability, expected change surface, deletions/additions/modifications, validation sequence and the canonical `order-tasks` block. `defect-plan-v2` substitutes root-cause/remediation and regression/preservation wording but is similarly narrower than S8-S10. The structural admission check proves only those reduced template headings, while PLAN promotion mechanically parses the `order-tasks` block. The canonical block is correctly singular and must remain the execution-task representation, but it does not by itself replace the surrounding S8-S10 plan obligations.

This is the same failure class previously exposed by G-F02 at DESIGN: productive authoring can omit content that later readiness authority expects. It is therefore a foundation defect, not a C09 product-design delta and not a reason to preserve or redesign the current UI.

**Required remediation boundary:**
- extend only the current Change/Defect v2 PLAN authoring/template contract so it explicitly carries the complete S8-S10 obligations;
- preserve all historical v1 PLAN templates/pins unchanged;
- keep the single canonical `order-tasks` representation; do not introduce another task DTO/model/block;
- do not move P1-P6 decisions into PLAN;
- align the current template owner/P6 documentation as required;
- strengthen PLAN conformance questions/admission only to the extent needed to prevent structurally incomplete S8-S10 output from promoting;
- add permanent regression coverage proving current-v2 PLAN completeness and historical/current separation.

**C09 historical R1 disposition:** `BLOCKED` until G-F04 remediation; resolved below on exact candidate `e91d3e5`.

**Closed C01-C08 impact:** none established. G-F04 concerns PLAN authoring/conformance; it does not change the P2/P3 authority or factual conclusions on which C01-C08 closed.

### CONCERN END: C09 — PLAN-stage interaction, task-definition authoring and plan inspection

**End baseline:** `e91d3e5e3d0164a79f657e0cff02fdc0ba46a096`

**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- PLAN receives P0, applicable P1-P6 authority, accepted DESIGN, relevant repository structure, project verification authority and minimum-change constraints. It may decompose work, order dependencies, forecast exact change surface/deletions/modifications and select task-specific verification sequence; it may not create product requirements, architecture, canonical contracts or scope.
- Every executable task must trace upward to resolved authority and every approved required outcome must trace downward to implementation or an explicit no-code disposition.
- The canonical `order-tasks` block is the single executable P7 task representation. PLAN promotion requires exactly one structurally valid block in the exact current PLAN bytes. Complete mechanically resolvable task-definition validation is intentionally owned by R3 before `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`, not duplicated at PLAN.
- After G-F04, current Change/Defect v2 PLAN authoring structurally carries the complete S8-S10 planning contract: task objective/dependencies/traceability, module ownership/decomposition constraints and architecture-feedback checkpoints where required, expected production/test-validation surfaces, deletions/replacements, completion evidence, minimum-change forecast, verification contract and the canonical task block.
- Project verification commands/environments remain project-integration authority; PLAN names logical gates and cannot substitute a cheaper/local execution mechanism.
- The Workbench treats Implementation plan as the primary governed document using the common pre-signoff interaction grammar. Structured task/change-surface summaries, when shown, are derived inspection and never a second plan authority.

**Findings:** NONE after resolved foundational finding G-F04.

### CONCERN END: C10 — R3 readiness review, adversarial challenge and return routing

**End baseline:** `e91d3e5e3d0164a79f657e0cff02fdc0ba46a096`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED_WITH_FINDING`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- R3 receives the complete relevant P1-P7 authority chain, applicable P0 controls, current repository evidence needed for freshness/feasibility, and earlier conformance evidence. It is an independent adversarial judgment and must treat R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN as untrusted.
- R3 may decide only readiness and the exact defective owning layer; it may not repair requirements, design or plan on behalf of those stages.
- `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` requires coherent/current/traceable authority, complete current lifecycle material, bidirectional traceability, canonical-owner uniqueness, zero unresolved material authority conflicts/decisions and evidence supporting the verdict.
- Complete mechanically resolvable signed-task validation now executes against the exact current PLAN/material closure before `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` promotion. The validation is deliberately conditional on that outcome so `RETURN_TO_*` remains available to route a defective package upstream.
- Canonical R3 completion outcomes route deterministically: `RETURN_TO_RESEARCH -> R1`, `RETURN_TO_REQUIREMENTS -> R2`, `RETURN_TO_DESIGN -> DESIGN`, `RETURN_TO_PLAN -> PLAN`; `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` alone advances to Approval eligibility. Blocking remains an operational state of the owning stage.
- Readiness Review is canonically Review-class and read-only. The required primary hierarchy is verdict/current readiness state, findings/defective owner, package material, evidence, then Run history, except when actual required input temporarily owns attention.

**Finding C10-F01 — Readiness verdict presentation projects the wrong semantic state.**

Canonical authority `GW-FR-221-READINESS-REVIEW-HIERARCHY` and Workbench §14.6 require the readiness verdict to dominate the Readiness Review. Implementation evidence in `ui/src/ReadinessWorkspace.tsx` shows the section labelled **Readiness verdict** renders `materialPresentationStateLabel(materialState)` (for example Current/Stale), which is document maturity/currentness rather than the canonical R3 readiness outcome. The governed R3 document is rendered below in the findings material, so the actual `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF | RETURN_TO_* | BLOCKED` judgment is not projected into the primary verdict slot.

This is an existing-authority implementation violation. It does not define current UI as a baseline and does not create a new product requirement/state model.

**Required R2 disposition:** `REFERENCE` — `GW-FR-221-READINESS-REVIEW-HIERARCHY` applies unchanged. The reconstructed functional design must expose the canonical readiness verdict as the primary readiness state and must not substitute material maturity for that verdict.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** C10-F01 may affect C17 current-attention/recovery presentation only if later investigation proves the same semantic substitution outside Readiness; no such cross-concern fact is asserted here.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `FACTS_CLOSED`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to R3 outcome semantics, readiness mechanical-validation timing, exact return routing, or `GW-FR-221` Readiness Review hierarchy.

- Only `R3 / READY_FOR_SIGNOFF` with current conformance may enter Approval; reaching Approval is not approval and execution authority exists only after explicit sign-off of the exact current package.
- The package binds the exact WorkItem source snapshot, baseline commit, signed PLAN task block, R3 root and the persisted transitive material dependency closure. P6 keeps one canonical signed-task representation rather than duplicating upstream authority into Approval fields.
- Sign-off re-resolves the exact package, checks the expected package hash and final repository baseline, verifies current source/material bytes, then atomically persists Approval, materialized execution tasks and the lifecycle transition.
- Material source/governance/verification change invalidates Approval. Stale Approval is not signable and reconciliation returns ownership to the earliest canonical changed-material owner rather than silently refreshing or locally repairing the package.
- Approval is canonically one Review-class workspace. Package meaning/currentness/readiness and signed task scope/gates dominate hashes/commit identity; explicit approver identity and sign-off remain adjacent to the exact judged package.

**Finding C11-F01 — Approval overview does not surface readiness as primary review state.**

`GW-DOC-WORKBENCH-UX` §14.7 requires the primary Approval hierarchy to begin with package currentness **and readiness**, followed by signed task meaning/scope/gates. Implementation evidence in `ui/src/SignoffReview.tsx` presents package currentness and task scope correctly, but it does not project the canonical R3 readiness judgment into the primary Approval overview; R3 is only indirectly available as governed package material.

This is an existing-authority presentation violation. It does not change sign-off semantics, package identity, task ownership or stale-authority routing.

**Required R2 disposition:** `REFERENCE` — existing Approval hierarchy authority applies unchanged. The reconstructed functional design must make exact package readiness/currentness directly legible in the primary Approval judgment surface without promoting hashes/ids or creating another readiness state.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** NONE. C10-F01 and C11-F01 are separate projections of the same canonical R3 outcome into their respective Review surfaces and do not require reopening C10 facts.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to sign-off package identity/freshness, explicit authorization semantics, stale-authority routing, or Approval Review hierarchy.
