### CONCERN END: C12 — Execution, task ownership, builder/reviewer/remediator progression and validation

**End baseline:** `64f36a85c4a8f554a40388d1a306d38b9f29beaa`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- Execution authority is derived only from explicit Approval and canonical signed task definitions; workers cannot create or expand tasks.
- Exactly one current execution owner is derived from current Approval/task/Run state. Manual Start targets that exact owner; the global scheduler remains a separate existing automatic-selection path rather than a second task authority.
- BUILDER, REVIEWER and REMEDIATOR Runs are durable role-specific execution records. Independent review cannot edit the candidate.
- V0-V7 is a real validation control plane. Candidate completion requires current evidence through every required boundary; builder self-attestation cannot substitute for validation.
- Repairable `TEST_FAILURE`, `REVIEW_FAILURE` and authorized `CHANGE_BLOAT` are bounded. Specification, stale approval, scope and governance failures return/block according to their canonical owner rather than being blindly repaired.
- Explicit Resume changes only the blocked current owner back to an eligible state after the blocker is resolved; it does not create a Run or advance lifecycle.
- Implementation is stable master/detail. Historical task/Run selection is inspection state and does not change current task ownership, blocker or primary action.

### CONCERN START: C15 — OpenCode/provider interaction, session continuity, observable activity, input and failure semantics

**Start baseline:** current campaign branch after C14 closure  
**Campaign phase:** R1 factual investigation

**Canonical owners:**
- `GW-DOC-OPENCODE-INTEGRATION-V1`
- `GW-DOC-WORKBENCH-UX`
- applicable lifecycle/run contracts.

**In scope:**
- provider/model selection and invocation boundary;
- session continuity/resume versus canonical Order state;
- observable Run activity and required human input;
- provider operational failure/replacement semantics;
- cancellation/interruption and durable event history;
- Workbench dialogue/activity presentation.

**Explicitly out of scope:** Settings configuration UX (C16), generic recovery precedence across all concerns (C17), and execution-task validation semantics already closed in C12.

**Questions to close:**
1. Is OpenCode an external execution/session mechanism rather than a canonical workflow/state owner?
2. Can session continuity resume productively without making session memory authoritative?
3. Are provider activity, required input and terminal events durable/observable in Order state?
4. Is provider failure replacement bounded and classified rather than unlimited retry?
5. Does cancellation preserve canonical lifecycle/task consequences?
6. What remaining authority or implementation violations require later remediation?


### CONCERN END: C15 — OpenCode/provider interaction, session continuity, observable activity, input and failure semantics

**End baseline:** `1387407fa695cf7b47b53e9796c95fd0fd428d3e`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- OpenCode is an execution provider only; it owns no workflow, task, approval, retry, project-context or completion authority.
- Every Order Run begins with a fresh provider session. Same-Run continuation is admitted only while stage/context/model/permissions/workspace identity remain current.
- Replacement sessions are reconstructed from current canonical Order context and persisted evidence; old conversation is evidence, not authority.
- Productive turns use deterministic prompt admission/correlation and Order-controlled completion reconciliation. Provider liveness/event streams are not completion authority.
- Provider-native activity is allowlisted/redacted into bounded coordinator-owned RunEvents. Missing live activity never changes Run correctness.
- Required human input and conversation are durable Order RunEvents. Cancellation and provider failure produce canonical lifecycle/task consequences rather than hidden session behavior.
- Provider-process loss is bounded: pre-signoff may replace from current context; post-signoff follows one bounded operational replacement policy and otherwise blocks.

**Findings:** NONE.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** NONE.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `FACTS_CLOSED`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to provider/session continuity, prompt admission/completion, activity projection, required input durability, cancellation or provider-failure replacement.

### CONCERN START: C16 — Settings, installation readiness, repositories/document roots, accounts, models and reference roots

**Start baseline:** current campaign branch after C15 closure  
**Campaign phase:** R1 factual investigation

**Canonical owners:**
- `GW-DOC-INSTALLATION-READINESS-V1`
- `GW-DOC-WORKBENCH-UX`
- applicable project-governance/provider integration authority.

**In scope:**
- productive readiness and setup status;
- exact target repositories and project-local documentation roots;
- reference roots and their read-only role;
- saved OpenCode accounts/login/switching/removal;
- exact provider/model selection;
- Settings interaction hierarchy and mutation safety.

**Explicitly out of scope:** provider turn/session semantics (C15), source reconciliation inside a selected project (C02-C04), and general current-attention/recovery semantics (C17).

**Questions to close:**
1. Does Settings expose only operator-owned productive configuration rather than duplicating workflow state?
2. Are target repositories exact Git roots and documentation roots project-local/deterministic?
3. Are reference roots clearly separate read-only context rather than project authority?
4. Are account/model changes explicit, persisted and safe against active productive use?
5. Does readiness identify concrete setup blockers without silently defaulting missing operator intent?
6. What implementation violations require later remediation?


### CONCERN END: C16 — Settings, installation readiness, repositories/document roots, accounts, models and reference roots

**End baseline:** `a186296c2a1657e13fc851dc402a0cc356f1a09e`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED_WITH_FINDING`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- Installation success and productive readiness are distinct; readiness is derived from canonical configuration/integration state and is not another lifecycle state.
- Settings is the single ordinary application-level owner for target repositories, OpenCode accounts and productive model/runtime selection. First-run/recovery routes into that same state rather than creating another configuration model.
- Target repositories are exact validated Git roots. Project-local document roots are repository-owned manifest state; moving an existing populated root is an explicit bounded migration, not a field edit.
- Reference roots are installation configuration for read-only STAGE context and never become target repositories or project authority.
- Saved OpenCode accounts remain OpenCode-owned credential state in Order's private namespace; account/model changes are explicit configuration actions.
- Missing repository/account/model readiness is surfaced before productive work rather than through downstream provider failure.

**Finding C16-F01 — Settings promotes technical filesystem/transport identity into ordinary primary content.**

`GW-DOC-INSTALLATION-READINESS-V1` §§7.2/7.5 require project identity, readiness and relevant actions to dominate connected-repository presentation while exact Git/document paths and diagnostic identifiers remain secondary unless needed to disambiguate. Current `ui/src/SettingsWorkspace.tsx` renders `Git root` and `WorkItem documents` as persistent primary repository-table columns and exposes the raw model-list HTTP source as ordinary supporting content.

This is an existing-authority information-hierarchy violation, not a configuration-contract defect.

**Required R2 disposition:** `REFERENCE` — existing Settings hierarchy applies unchanged. The reconstructed functional design must keep operator decisions/readiness primary and move exact paths/API identity into secondary/on-demand technical detail except where needed for the active configuration task.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** C16-F01 may inform C19 presentation consistency, but C19 must independently determine whether the same technical-detail promotion is systemic.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `GAP_FOUND`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to installation-readiness ownership, repository/document-root configuration, account/model configuration, reference-root semantics or Settings hierarchy.

### CONCERN END: C17 — Current attention, blocking, errors, stale authority, retry/resume/recovery and asynchronous convergence

**End baseline:** `03bb8d305f9e3d1d7095f46a1de5ca2bff301ce5`  
**R1 verdict:** `VERIFIED`.

**Authority facts / decision inputs established:**
- Current attention is selected from one closed precedence language in the functional core: convergence, mutation conflict, required input, proposal decision/adoption, blocker, stale authority, recovery, processing, actionable, ordinary.
- Recovery is separately closed to `NONE | RETRY_CURRENT_STAGE | RESUME_CURRENT_OWNER | RECONCILE_AUTHORITY`; stale authority specifically maps to reconciliation rather than generic Resume.
- Observation coherence is evaluated before actions. Canonically inconsistent dependent observations derive `CONVERGING` and suppress workflow mutations until refetch converges.
- Historical material selection does not alter current attention, execution owner or recovery. The shell preserves the current attention signal/action while historical detail remains inspectable.
- Mutation conflicts, proposal ownership and active Runs suppress lower-priority competing mutations.
- C10-F01 is local to Readiness verdict presentation; no broader C17 semantic-state substitution was found.

**Findings:** NONE.

**Cross-concern findings emitted:** NONE.

**Incoming findings resolved:** C10-F01 does not establish a C17-wide state-model defect.

**Open product decisions for R2:** NONE.

**Open factual questions:** NONE.

**R1 disposition:** `FACTS_CLOSED`.

**Revalidation trigger:** changes to current-attention precedence, recovery derivation, observation coherence, mutation serialization or historical-selection/current-owner separation.
